- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are William, leader of The Netherlands (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Netherlands
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Sea Beggar, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Unit: Goedendag, Replacing Pikeman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Doelen, Replacing Constabulary (Requires technology: Banking)
        - Unique Improvement: Polder (Can only be built on Marsh tiles or on featureless Lake/Coast tiles adjacent to at least 3 land tiles. Polders built on water can be traversed by land units without embarkation, as if they are land tiles.); Requires technology: Guilds
        - Civilization Ability: +4 Gold for each unique Luxury Resource you import or export from/to other Civilizations and City-States, scaling with Era. Can import duplicate Luxuries from Civilizations which count toward Monopolies. +1 Gold and Culture from Luxury Resources.
      - Leader: William
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 8
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 1
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 5
        - WarBias: 3
        - HostileBias: 6
        - WarmongerHate: 5

        - DenounceWillingness: 3
        - Forgiveness: 7
        - Meanness: 1
        - Neediness: 6
        - Chattiness: 8
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Turn 466: MAINTAINING ALL PERSONA VALUES. Maximum MinorCivCompetitiveness (10) and DiplomaticBalance (10) essential for securing Aksum delegate when Open Door passes (1 turn). Low WarBias (3) critical for defensive posture against Persia's 4.5:1 military advantage. High Loyalty (10) maintains Huns defensive pact coordination against shared Persian threat. No changes needed - values remain optimal for survival strategy.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Radar
        - Rationale: Turn 472: CONFIRMING Radar as next research. With Manhattan Project completed, nuclear deterrent is available, but immediate air defense remains critical against Persia's expanding air force (now 5 visible air units: 3 Triplanes, 2 Bombers in Zone 1060). Radar unlocks Fighters (60 ranged strength) for air defense and Heavy Bombers for retaliation. With Persia actively building more aircraft, Fighter deployment is essential to protect Bremen.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Total War (Policy)
        - Rationale: Turn 472: CONFIRMING Total War as next policy. Morocco's entry into war against Persia/Japan provides relief, but I still face direct Persian attack with 4.5:1 force disadvantage. +25% Production for Land Units essential for building nuclear weapons (now possible with Manhattan Project) and defensive units. Total War synergizes with Mobilization flavor for maximum military output from single city.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Eraclea
        - Public: 95
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: Eraclea: Alliance confirmed (375 influence, up from 307). Spy Agent Oom successfully rigging elections. Protestantism converted. Maintaining maximum relationship for delegate security and Science from Shadow Networks (+25% from allied city-states).
        - UpdatedTurn: 200
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: 70
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: Sokoto: New peace established (events 28-30). Formerly at war, now neutral. They have a barbarian horde quest (9 turns) and are allied with Polynesia (104 influence). Moving spy Agent Hannes there to rig elections. Maintaining positive relationship to eventually flip this Polynesian ally - would be a significant blow to their 11 delegates.
        - UpdatedTurn: 222
        
        ### Shedet
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Shedet relationship to neutral. Germany captured and liberated Shedet (revoking our protection), now we have 45 influence vs 0 for others. Not immediately pursuable due to liberation cooldown and other priorities (Aksum is easier). Will revisit later - not worth negative modifiers when we can't act on it yet.
        - UpdatedTurn: 295
        
        ### Melbourne
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Melbourne: Gap of 244 (Huns 423 vs us 179) cannot be closed in 7 turns. Resources better spent on Quebec City where we have a realistic path to Ally status. Reset to neutral to focus AI spending on achievable targets.

        ### Quebec City

        - Rationale: Quebec City: RESET TO NEUTRAL. With only 6 turns until World Congress and Germany leading 629 to 34 influence, achieving Ally status is mathematically impossible even with maximum envoy investment. Germany has 1900 gold to maintain lead. Conserving 29k gold treasury for Aksum (achievable) and next Congress cycle is optimal strategy.

With Decolonization in 8 turns resetting all influence to 40, I can compete for it again then. No point maintaining high relationship modifiers now when I cannot act.

        - UpdatedTurn: 413

Conserve diplomatic capital for Aksum competition and post-World Congress opportunities. Agent Caspar better redeployed if rigging ineffective.
        - UpdatedTurn: 449
        
        ### Heidabir
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Heidabir: CRITICAL UPDATE - Became ally through spy election rigging (Event 56, Turn 450), providing 9th delegate. Under heavy Moroccan military assault (battleships, special forces visible in Zone 1068). City health at 45%, taking damage from naval bombardment. Maximum priority essential to signal AI to defend this delegate at all costs. Losing Heidabir means losing World Congress leverage against Freedom bloc's 48 delegates. Agent Willemina successfully rigged election for +180 influence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 451

        - Public: 70
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: Turn 458: Japan now more important - fellow Autocracy ally (though Order ideology?). Previously at 100/100 but need to verify if they still exist independently. Persia's vassal state (captured Kyoto). Reduced to 70/65 - still friendly but acknowledging their vassal status limits independence. Need their 3 delegates if they can vote independently.
        - UpdatedTurn: 458
        
        ### Germany
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -65
        - Rationale: Turn 463: INCREASED Germany hostility from -50/-55 to -60/-65. Persia made peace with Germany (Events 0-25), ending conflict that tied up German forces. Combined with Germany's 20009 military strength and my 1924, existential threat level has RISEN. They have my capital (Amsterdam), propose World Ideology Freedom (opposing my Autocracy), and their 29 delegates threaten Diplomatic Victory. Maximum hostility signals that no cooperation possible - they are existential enemy. Reduced from -100 only because cannot fight them directly with 10:1 disadvantage.
        - UpdatedTurn: 463
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: Persia declared WAR on me and The Huns (Events 72-74, 80). This is an existential threat - Persia has 8777 military strength vs my 1771 (5:1 ratio). They control Japan as a vassal and have dragged their allied city-states (Lutetia, Kwa-Bulawayo, Bornu, Bogota) into war against me. With only 33 turns until Nuclear Fission and game ending soon, this war threatens my survival. Maximum hostility signals the AI to prioritize defensive actions and coordinate with Huns defensive pact ally.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: MAINTAIN Aztecs at maximum 100/100. Fellow Autocracy ally now EVEN MORE CRITICAL - they are at war with Polynesia but may be needed against Persia if they expand aggression. Their 13831 military strength is vital counterweight to Persia's 8777. With Persia-Germany peace consolidating threats and now Persia attacking me, Aztec support is essential.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: MAINTAIN Huns at maximum 100/100 - NOW UNDER ATTACK BY PERSIA TOO. Defensive Pact is ACTIVE and being tested. Persia declared war on both of us (Events 72, 80). Combined our military (1771 + 3380 = 5151) still inferior to Persia's 8777, but defensive pact may deter full invasion. Maximum relationship ensures coordinated defense and prevents separate peace.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### Bornu
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: Bornu NOW HOSTILE - they joined Persia's war against me (Events 75, 78). Lost 205 influence, now at -60. They are at war with me through their Persian alliance. Maximum negative relationship signals AI to treat as enemy combatant - block their units, don't waste diplomatic resources trying to flip them while at war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### Lutetia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: Lutetia NOW HOSTILE - declared war on me as Persian ally (Event 75). They were Persian SOI, now active combatant. Maximum hostility required - do not attempt diplomacy while at war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### Kwa-Bulawayo
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: Kwa-Bulawayo NOW HOSTILE - declared war on me as Persian ally (Event 76). Persian SOI city-state now active combatant. Maximum hostility for war duration.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### Bogota
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 465: Bogota NOW HOSTILE - declared war on me as Persian ally (Event 79). Was Persian SOI at peace, now active combatant. Maximum hostility for war duration.
        - UpdatedTurn: 465
        
        ### Jakarta
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Turn 468: Jakarta remains Germany's ally (866 influence vs my 208) with overwhelming German economic dominance. With Persian war consuming all defensive resources and Manhattan Project 4 turns from completion, spending diplomatic capital here is futile. Reset to neutral to conserve gold for Aksum (Open Door imminent) and emergency military purchases. German economic lock on Jakarta is unbreakable.
        - UpdatedTurn: 468
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 70
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: Turn 469: INCREASING Polynesia relationship from 0/0 to 70/65 (Friendly). CRITICAL POSITIVE SHIFT: Events 18-21 show Aztecs vassalized Polynesia, and Event 39 shows Polynesia switched to Autocracy ideology (from Freedom). They are now aligned with my ideology and vassalized by my strongest ally (Aztecs). This eliminates a Freedom delegate (Germany had 28, now potentially 27) and creates potential Autocracy bloc coordination. Maintaining friendly relations ensures trade route benefits (Open Borders active) and potential World Congress cooperation. Their 9 delegates now effectively support Aztec interests which align with mine.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Turn 472: INCREASING Morocco from 30/25 to 50/45.         - VotesNeeded: 44

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Sanction: Japan:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Persian Sphere of Influence: Bogota:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Catholicism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Persian Sphere of Influence: Kwa-Bulawayo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Aksum:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Quebec City:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Persian Sphere of Influence: Lutetia:

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1

    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.

      # Your Civilization

      - Leader: William

      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Tradition (New Branch):

        - Adopting Tradition grants:

      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Domination Progress**
            
            I'm assessing our current domination standing. Germany holds Berlin and Amsterdam, Persia has Persepolis and Kyoto, and the Aztecs possess Tenochtitlan and Honolulu. This indicates significant territorial control by these factions.
            
            **Evaluating Victory Threats**
            
            My focus has shifted to imminent victory conditions. Morocco's cultural influence is a significant concern, as they are close to achieving a cultural win. Diplomatic maneuvering is also critical, with upcoming World Congress votes and Germany's strong delegate count.
            
            **Assessing Power Discrepancy**
            
            I'm observing our current position. Our cities are few, our military thin, and we've lost key territory. However, our substantial gold reserves and happy populace offer opportunities for strategic investment and expansion. Germany and Morocco boast overwhelming military might, a stark contrast to our current standing.
            
            **Revising War Status**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current conflicts. It appears I mistakenly believed we were at war with Germany, but our past capture of their capital was resolved peacefully. My current understanding is that we are at war only with Persia and Japan. Bremen is currently occupied, which requires immediate attention.
            
            **Clarifying Alliances**
            
            I'm untangling our war status. It seems The Huns' ongoing war with Germany, despite our defensive pact, means we aren't automatically at war with Germany. This is complex, especially as a German declaration on The Huns would pull us in. I'm also reviewing policy options for the next two turns.
            
            **Considering Policy Options**
            
            My thoughts are now focused on our next policy card, specifically `Total War`. Given our limited military and single city, its production bonus for land units seems less impactful. I'm weighing this against the potential of using our significant gold reserves to acquire city-states, or perhaps investing in science to pivot our grand strategy away from a distant spaceship victory, exploring diplomatic avenues instead.
            
            **Analyzing Victory Threats**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our immediate threats. Germany is on the cusp of a diplomatic victory, needing only a few more delegates, while Morocco is alarmingly close to a cultural win. Their military strength is overwhelming, making direct confrontation against Morocco impossible. My focus is now on disrupting Germany's city-state alliances.
            
            **Disrupting Diplomatic Dominance**
            
            I've discovered a critical opportunity. Germany's immense influence over city-states, a key component of their impending diplomatic victory, could be shattered by the "Decolonization" proposal. If this passes, their city-state delegate advantage would plummet, fundamentally altering the diplomatic landscape.
            
            **Considering Nuclear Strategy**
            
            I've realized we possess the uranium and research capability for atomic bombs. This presents a potent, albeit ethically complex, option to disrupt Morocco's cultural surge by targeting Germany's cities, should they be allied. However, the immediate voting period for "Decolonization" is paramount to counter Germany's diplomatic advantage.
            
            **Evaluating Nuclear Feasibility**
            
            I'm assessing the viability of a nuclear strategy. While we have the uranium and research capacity for atomic bombs, I've recognized the significant ethical implications and potential for indiscriminate harm. My priority remains disrupting Germany's diplomatic advantage through the "Decolonization" vote, as our current military weakness makes a direct nuclear confrontation with Morocco unfeasible and risky.
            
            **Utilizing Trade Routes**
            
            I've realized we have four unused trade routes, which is a significant untapped resource, especially for the Netherlands. Acquiring the "Computers" technology will grant us an additional trade route, further amplifying our economic potential through luxury goods. This seems a far more strategic choice than "Alternative Energy" or even "Radar" at this moment.
            
            **Choosing Next Tech**
            
            My current focus is on selecting our next technological advancement. While "Radar" is the immediate next step, I'm evaluating whether switching to "Computers" would be more beneficial given our Dutch abilities and the desire to increase trade routes for gold and science. I'm also considering the turn estimates for each path.
            
            **Deciding Next Policy**
            
            I'm now analyzing our next policy card, with "Total War" no longer seeming optimal due to our single city and limited land units. I'm weighing the benefits of "Police State" against the potential of other Level 2 Autocracy options, considering our current infrastructure and strategic goals.
            
            **Selecting Policy Benefits**
            
            I am prioritizing policy cards that offer the most strategic advantage. "Third Alternative" is appealing for resource doubling and unit maintenance reduction. However, "Mare Nostrum" presents a compelling production and culture boost to my sole city due to coastal tiles, potentially accelerating my science trajectory for a spaceship victory.
            
            **Prioritizing Scientific Advancement**
            
            I'm analyzing our path to victory. Generating Great Scientists faster seems paramount. I'm comparing "Free Thought" for its scientist boost against "Artistry" for its culture generation from Great Works to counter Morocco's tourism. The production and happiness bonuses from "Entrepreneurship" are also under consideration.
            
            **Considering Cultural Interference**
            
            I'm re-evaluating how to counter Morocco's imminent cultural victory. While boosting my own culture with "Artistry" seemed logical, it won't reverse their existing influence. Direct warfare is a possibility to disrupt trade, but I need to check if Germany is still a viable target for influencing them away from Morocco.
            
            **Exploring Diplomatic Ruptures**
            
            I'm examining ways to shatter existing alliances, specifically the strong ties between Germany and Morocco. Bribing them into conflict appears challenging due to their pacts. However, observing current wars involving Persia, Japan, and The Huns against these powers, I'm considering how our defensive pact with The Huns might create an opening.
            
            **Selecting Policy Benefits**
            
            I am focusing on maximizing our scientific output. Despite "Mare Nostrum's" production and culture bonuses, "Free Thought" offers a superior speed increase for Great Scientists, which is crucial for our science-focused victory condition.
            
            **Adjusting Strategy Priorities**
            
            I'm shifting our focus from nuclear capabilities to conventional strength and scientific advancement. I'm significantly reducing our inclination towards nukes while prioritizing defense, science, growth, and tile improvements. Production and Great People generation are also being boosted to accelerate our path to a science victory.
            
            **Revising War Declarations**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current war status with Japan. Despite being actively at war and losing badly, our relationship modifiers are surprisingly positive. I realize now these are manual settings intended to encourage peace, which is exactly what I need to pursue given the current war score.
            
            **Ending Diplomatic Deadlock**
            
            I'm exploring ways to break existing alliances. Germany and Morocco have a defensive pact, making direct conflict unlikely. However, observing other ongoing wars, I'm considering if their pact can be disrupted, potentially creating an opening for us.
            
            **Seeking Diplomatic Breakthrough**
            
            I'm currently analyzing the World Congress resolutions, specifically the "Passport System" which has been enacted and is significantly boosting Morocco's tourism. My immediate focus is on finding a way to repeal this resolution, as it represents the most direct path to disrupting Morocco's impending cultural victory and breaking this diplomatic deadlock.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Paradox**
            
            I'm re-examining Morocco's cultural victory path, specifically their rapid influence with Germany. It seems Morocco's tourism output far exceeds Germany's cultural output, a counterintuitive but critical observation that necessitates a revised strategy, moving away from repealing "Passport System" this turn.
            
            **Investigating Diplomatic Exploits**
            
            I'm probing how Morocco is achieving such immense tourism against Germany. Their output, amplified by numerous modifiers, is overwhelming. The key to disrupting this seems to be forcing Germany and Morocco into conflict, thereby severing their open borders and trade routes, but their defensive pact makes direct provocation difficult.
            
            **Prioritizing Defensive Research**
            
            I'm reaffirming our research path towards Radar. The ability to field Fighters and Heavy Bombers is crucial for securing Bremen and bolstering our overall defense, aligning with our current strategic posture.
            
            **Adjusting Research Priorities**
            
            I'm confirming our next research technology. Radar remains the priority for defensive upgrades, with Computers a potential follow-up. I'm also adjusting our game flavors to reflect an ethical stance against nuclear weapons and to boost growth, tile improvements, Great People generation, and production in Bremen.
            
            **Improving Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I'm focusing on improving our diplomatic relationships to extricate ourselves from costly distant wars. I'm substantially increasing our standing with Japan and Persia, both of whom we are currently at war with but are geographically remote. This is intended to facilitate peace negotiations and reduce drain on our resources.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Focus**
            
            I'm now prioritizing science and growth over nuclear capabilities. My research is focused on Radar for defense, with Computers as a follow-up. I'm also boosting growth, tile improvements, and production in Bremen, while setting positive relationships with Japan and Persia to end costly wars.
            
            **Prioritizing Relationship Improvements**
            
            I'm evaluating our diplomatic options, specifically how to extricate ourselves from detrimental wars. My immediate focus is on improving relations with Japan, Player 4, due to our current negative war score. I will use the `set-relationship` tool for Japan first, and then assess if I can also improve ties with Persia.
            
            **Verifying Player IDs**
            
            I'm confirming the player IDs for Japan and Persia to accurately target our diplomatic improvements. Japan is Player 4, led by Oda Nobunaga, and Persia is Player 3, led by Darius I. I need to prioritize which relationship to improve first, focusing on Japan due to our current war score.
            
            **Adjusting Game Flavors**
            
            I'm refining our game flavors to reflect a shift away from nuclear capabilities towards scientific dominance and ethical defense. My focus is on boosting growth, tile improvements, production, and Great Scientist generation, while significantly reducing Nuke and UseNuke flavors. This is to accelerate our domestic development and maintain a strong conventional defense, all while adhering to our Spaceship Grand Strategy.
            
            **Boosting Scientific Momentum**
            
            My focus is on accelerating our Great Scientist generation with "Free Thought," crucial for our spaceship victory. I'm also improving relations with Japan to exit our losing war. Radar remains our research priority for defense, and I'm watching Germany's delegate count closely for their diplomatic victory threat.
            
            **Voting for Decolonization**
            
            I'm preparing to vote for the "Decolonization" resolution, which Persia has proposed. This is critical to counter Germany's diplomatic victory by resetting their city-state influence. I've adjusted our flavors to align with this, prioritizing Diplomacy, and will ensure we vote for it in five turns.
            
            **Prioritizing Peace Negotiations**
            
            I'm focusing on extricating us from our losing war with Japan by setting maximum friendly relationships. While I also want to improve ties with Persia, I need to confirm if multiple `set-relationship` calls are supported to avoid overwriting. My primary goal is ending the detrimental war score.
